# Test Design: Epic 1, The Evaluate authoring loop

**Date:** 2026-09-22
**Author:** eval-testdesign (planning fleet step 4), on the owner's behalf
**Status:** Draft for owner review

## Executive Summary

**Scope:** full epic-level test design for Stories 1.1 to 1.78, 1.80 to 1.89, 1.90 to 1.116, 1.130 to 1.132 of `epics.md`. The 2026-09-23 amendment added Stories 1.17 to 1.26 and extended Stories 1.3, 1.4, 1.12, 1.13, 1.14 and 1.16 to close the plan gap audit; later story findings appended Stories 1.27 to 1.78, 1.80 to 1.89, 1.90 to 1.116, 1.130 to 1.132. Their scenarios, risks and gates are below, and sections appear in execution order. Every acceptance criterion here names the check that fails when its story's work is reverted. A worker treats this file and `epics.md` together as the story's test plan.

**Risk summary:**

- Risks identified: 37
- High-priority risks (score 6 or more): 21, two of them at 9 as the stories were first written (R1-21, R1-22), each closed by a corrected acceptance criterion
- Dominant categories: TECH (engine boundary, runtime moves, evaluator isolation, shape tests), DATA (rollback, evidence and held-out integrity) and BUS (guidance that looks complete and produces weak evaluations)

**Coverage summary:**

The scenario counts below are the initial planning estimate. Appended stories are itemized in their sections and have not been re-estimated.

- P0: 81 scenarios over the runtime's integrity paths (rollback, verdict source, infrastructure classification, evaluator isolation, held-out redaction, calibration, the dogfood proof, the behavioral proofs of guidance)
- P1: 67 scenarios over authoring checks, adapters, registration, the evaluation layer and craft headings
- P2: 12 scenarios over guidance text, packaging and documentation counts
- New `npm test` scripts: `test:trial-set-scoring`, `test:evaluate-boundaries`, `test:evaluate-check`, `test:evaluate-preflight`, `test:evaluate-mutation`, `test:evaluate-run`, `test:evaluate-arms`, `test:evaluate-evaluators`, `test:evaluate-mcp`, `test:evaluate-api`, `test:evaluate-workflow`, `test:evaluate-tool-use`, `test:evaluate-promptfoo`, `test:evaluate-partitions`, `test:evaluate-calibration`, `test:evaluate-interpret`, `test:evaluate-guidance`, `test:evaluate-authoring`, `test:evaluate-gap-loop`, `test:evaluate-learned-framework`, each chained into `npm test` (amended 2026-09-25 in Story 1.9: CI runs the `npm test` chain in shards through the `chain` matrix, which `test:ci-coverage` and `test:shards` hold, so a chained script needs no step of its own)

**Corrections made to `epics.md` in this step:** acceptance criteria in fourteen stories were untestable or wrong as written. Each is listed with its reason under "Acceptance Criteria Corrected In epics.md" and the corrected text is already in `epics.md`.

## Test Levels Used In This Epic

TeA's suites are plain Node scripts using `node:assert`, a header comment stating what the file proves, a non-zero exit on the first failed assertion, and one `npm run test:<name>` script chained into `npm test`. Every new file here follows that house form. `tools/validate-ci-coverage.js` fails any chained script that neither the `chain` matrix runs nor a workflow step names, so each new script lands in the chain in the same story (amended 2026-09-25 in Story 1.9: CI runs the `npm test` chain in shards through the `chain` matrix, which `test:ci-coverage` and `test:shards` hold, so a chained script needs no step of its own).

| Level                              | What it means here                                                                                                                              | Example                                                                                                                       |
| ---------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Unit                               | A pure function in `cli/lib/evaluate/` exercised in process with no engine call and no child process                                            | `corpus-index.json` sorting, AD-7 `conditionArm` labels                                                                       |
| Static                             | A source scan over committed files that holds a structural rule (imports, allowed engine symbols, forbidden literals)                           | `test:evaluate-boundaries`, the HTTP port grep                                                                                |
| Integration over real eval-quality | The runtime drives the installed `eval-quality` CLI and library on fixture files, with stub targets under `test/fixtures/`                      | `test:evaluate-run` end to end on a stub target                                                                               |
| Contract                           | An artifact validated against eval-quality's published schemas or the runtime-owned schemas                                                     | `evaluation.json` template against the runtime schema                                                                         |
| Guidance                           | A structured read of prompt content in the skill (`SKILL.md`, `references/`, `assets/`) that fails when a required passage or marker is removed | `test:evaluate-guidance`                                                                                                      |
| Replay                             | Stored observations and sealed records re-scored through the CLI with no target launch                                                          | Story 1.2's `test:trial-set-scoring`, which re-scores stored replay records as a three-trial set through `eval-quality score` |
| Live                               | A real agent run of a skill, recorded as evidence; Story 1.24 uses isolated `gpt-6-sol` high sessions under its amended AC                      | Story 1.16's proof run; the authoring, gap-loop and learned-framework sessions of Stories 1.24 to 1.26                        |
| Behavioral guidance                | A live authoring session whose committed output is then re-run deterministically in `npm test` and must score strong                            | `test:evaluate-authoring`, `test:evaluate-gap-loop`, `test:evaluate-learned-framework`                                        |

Live runs never enter `npm test`. Their evidence is recorded and then held by deterministic checks.

## Risk Assessment

Scores use probability (1 to 3) times impact (1 to 3). Six or more requires a mitigation that lands in the story named; nine would block release. Owner for every mitigation is the `/bmad-build` worker of the story named in the Timeline column, verified by the coordinator's review layers.

## NFR Planning

| NFR category    | Requirement                                                                                                                         | Risk link                  | Planned validation                                                                                           | Evidence                                                                          |
| --------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Security        | NFR3 (`web` never emitted), NFR4 (signature channels), AD-20 (only declared environment keys reach a target, `PATH` never declared) | R1-12, R1-13               | `test:evaluate-check` cases; command conformance's environment-key denial through `createCommandLineAdapter` | `npm test` output                                                                 |
| Reliability     | AD-8 rollback proof; AD-7 infrastructure classification                                                                             | R1-01, R1-10, R1-11        | `test:evaluate-mutation`, `test:evaluate-run`                                                                | `npm test` output; Story 1.16 rollback digests                                    |
| Maintainability | NFR1 single engine, AD-5 single driver, NFR6 floating versions, NFR10 framework-neutral runtime                                     | R1-02, R1-06, R1-07, R1-26 | `test:evaluate-boundaries`; Story 1.15 float; empty `cli/` diffs in Stories 1.20 and 1.26                    | `npm test` output; `git diff -- package.json package-lock.json`; completion notes |
| Performance     | UNKNOWN: no threshold stated for `npm test` duration or live run cost                                                               | R1-20                      | Measured and recorded per story                                                                              | Completion notes                                                                  |

Unknown threshold: no source states a limit on `npm test` wall time or on live proof duration. Recorded as unknown; stories report measured values.

## Entry Criteria

- [ ] The engine check exits 0 in the TeA worktree on the published eval-quality package TeA's devDependency resolves (4.0.0 or later, carrying Story 1.1's export and trial-set scoring)
- [ ] `npm test` is green on the TeA worktree before Story 1.2's change (the baseline every later failure is compared with)
- [ ] The local Claude Code CLI runs non-interactively on the build machine (needed by Stories 1.3 and 1.16)

## Exit Criteria

- [ ] Every P0 and P1 scenario below exists and passes in `npm test`
- [ ] Every acceptance criterion's revert check was exercised once by the worker (revert the change locally, observe the named failure, restore) and the observation is in the completion notes
- [ ] Story 1.16's proof record holds every item in "The Dogfood Proof" below
- [ ] The committed outputs of Stories 1.24, 1.25 and 1.26 reach their defined strong states in `test:evaluate-authoring`, `test:evaluate-gap-loop` and `test:evaluate-learned-framework`, and each session's transcript shows no read outside its permitted folder
- [ ] `git diff -- package.json package-lock.json` shows no `file:` or `.tgz` spec

| 1.2                                | `test/test-schema-versions.js` reads each constant from the installed package, so it cannot fail on 3.4.0 because of the repair; `test/test-eval-quality-corpus.js` feeds the package nothing of TeA's by design                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | A new `test:trial-set-scoring` that fails on published 3.4.0; the tests the engine change broke are named as the repair's revert checks                                                                                                                                                 |
| 1.3                                | The EV intent changes `intents.json`, which `test:eval-routing-evidence` holds to Story 1.3's live evidence by case ids and whole-file digest, so the story could not finish green; `bmad-teach-me-testing` has no `workflow.yaml` and does have `steps-c/`; `persistent_facts = []` and `on_complete` live in `customize.toml`; the guidance test had no owner until Story 1.12                                                                                                            | Per-case snapshots with new per-case digests and a recorded live routing run; lean defined as no `workflow.yaml` and no `steps-c/`; activation assertions split by file; `test:evaluate-guidance` created and chained here                                                              |
| 1.4                                | The AD-14 case needs TeA-only knowledge the shipped runtime cannot have; code moved into `cli/` requires `ajv`, a devDependency, so the runtime would crash for adopters; the `cli` layer has no externals list, so "one allowed external" would fail `test:direction` or check nothing; a required peer is auto-installed by npm 7 and later; "exactly one `import('eval-quality'` site" cannot hold once Story 1.5 moves the `eval-quality/adapters` import and the synchronous `require` | AD-14 moved to Story 1.15; `ajv` in `dependencies` with a packed-install case; a full externals list; optional peer; one engine module `engine.js` with a test seam                                                                                                                     |
| 1.5                                | "pass unchanged in their assertions" still passes after a revert; a text scan for `createCommandLineAdapter(` or `repositoryState(` hits legal call sites across `test/`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Definition scans in named files                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| 1.6                                | The existing runners exit 2 for a usage error, so "2 to 6 for infrastructure" misclassifies it; a byte comparison with the CLI cannot tell the CLI verdict from the identical library verdict                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Codes 3 to 6 plus usage 2; a logging engine shim                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

| 1.13                               | "once filled from the example" had no mechanical meaning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | A fill-values file and a failing unfilled-placeholder case                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| 1.15                               | `evalType` is a closed enum in `test/schema/suite-manifest.js`, `eval:all` spawns every suite's harness, and three counters count only `behavioral`, so Story 1.16's registration would leave the skill unaccounted                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Schema branch, `eval:all` routing and counters named, with a revert case; AD-14 case added                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| 1.3 to 1.16 (amendment 2026-09-23) | The plan gap audit found eleven partial and two missing items: guides specified as term lists, no evaluation-layer choice, no workflow or calling-agent fixture, no held-out probes or judge calibration, no `seal` in the skill stage, fixed CI membership                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Stories 1.3, 1.4, 1.12, 1.13, 1.14 and 1.16 extended; Stories 1.17 to 1.26 added; scenarios below                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| 1.16                               | TEA is not installed into its own repository, so the activation contract had no config to load; the behavior's channel was unconstrained; a multiply-stated behavior may not manifest under a one-file mutation; the worktree `shasum` proved an untouched tree and was labelled rollback proof                                                                                                                                                                                             | Setup steps; stdout or exit code; single-source output with a manifestation check; the independent CLI re-score; relabelled check                                                                                                                                                       |

## Coverage Plan By Story

Each table row is one acceptance criterion. "Revert check" names the test and what it observes when the story's change is undone. Priority follows `test-priorities-matrix.md`.

### Story 1.25: Close seeded weaknesses through the gap loop

File: `test/test-evaluate-gap-loop.js` (`test:evaluate-gap-loop`).

| AC                                                                                                                                              | Test                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Level         | P   | Revert check                                                     |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------- | --- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Before evidence shows W1 (P-007 first qualification stop, then P-009 gameability qualification failure) and W2 (`malformed-input` coverage gap) | Recompute P-009 qualification from the committed degenerate response and seeded contract; replay the scored held-out partition through direct `preflight` and `score`, byte-equal to committed evidence; compare the redacted coverage summary to the scored artifact | Replay        | P0  | A before copy without either seeded weakness fails the assertion |
| Both after partitions strong                                                                                                                    | Direct preflight and score replay of development and held-out inputs, byte-equal to committed evidence                                                                                                                                                                | Replay        | P0  | Reverting the tightened oracle changes the evidence and fails    |
| Every changed file is named in the gap report                                                                                                   | Diff `before/` against `after/`; parse the committed gap report                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Static        | P1  | An unexplained edit fails                                        |
| Session never read `SEEDED.md` or held-out probe files                                                                                          | Transcript file reads cited in completion notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Live evidence | P0  | Recorded evidence                                                |

W2's seed removes `typed-file`, the sole type-violating `review` interaction, together with the O-004 direction and check pointers that address it. W1's loose O-003 also lets P-007's mutation pass, so the full development run stops before P-009. The focused P-009 diagnostic is separate from the scored held-out partition. The latter records W2's gap, while the blind maintainer receives only its redacted rule summary. A scored before development verdict is unavailable.

| AC                                               | Test                                                                                                                                           | Level                              | P   | Revert check                                                                           |
| ------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------- | --- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Ungraded framework error stops as infrastructure | Feed a JSONL row with an `error`, target stdout and no `gradingResult` through fixture and starter; `run` exits 12 with no sealed trial record | Integration over real eval-quality | P0  | Restoring the Story 1.20 fail-row conversion creates a target finding or sealed record |
| Graded assertion outcomes retain their meaning   | A `pass: false` component emits a cited target fail; a `pass: true` component emits pass; run both through the fixture and rendered starter    | Integration                        | P0  | Rejecting graded failures or passing them changes the expected rows                    |
| Grade set is complete and unique                 | Missing, duplicate and malformed components stop the trial with no record, including a partial grade set where one assertion succeeded         | Unit, integration                  | P1  | Permitting a partial set writes a record                                               |
| Guide names the failure boundary                 | Assert the evaluator guide distinguishes ungraded framework errors from graded target failures by exact section and runnable examples          | Guidance                           | P1  | Removing the distinction fails the guidance assertion                                  |

The installed promptfoo grades a thrown assertion as a failing component, so a thrown `javascript` assertion or a crashing code file stays a target `fail` row under this story; Story 1.70 closes it (added 2026-10-01; closed in Story 1.70's build, 2026-10-02: both evaluators now refuse such an assertion).

Amended 2026-10-01 in the Story 1.44 build. The integration cases run in `test/test-evaluate-evaluators.js` (group `agents`: `checkInstalledFrameworks` and `checkInstalledFrameworksMidRun`, over a command-evaluator project whose package `probe-fw` is installed under the project's own `node_modules`; the repository's is read only), the contract rows in `test/test-evaluate-check.js` (`FRAMEWORK_CASES` and the clean cases), the configuration row as a unit in `checkUnits`, and the starter rows in `test/test-evaluate-guidance.js` (the rendered AgentEvals and promptfoo starters run `check`, `run` and `score` with their declaration template, the shipped probe and a filled `LEARNED.md`, and the run's `framework-versions.json` names the installed version). A mid-run change is made by the evaluator itself (`--mode bump-package`) and by a target between two trials (`VERDICT_DO=bump`, an unconfined run); stale documentation is a declaration newer than `LEARNED.md`, which `check` and `run` refuse with exit 10.

### Story 1.45: Aggregate development strength through eval-quality

Added 2026-09-28 in Story 1.14's final review. Levels: integration over a published eval-quality release, replay, and guidance. The runtime currently scores one probe per engine call, so this story must obtain a class-wide result from the engine and retain its lineage.

| AC                                     | Test                                                                                                                                                                         | Level                              | P   | Revert check                                                                  |
| -------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------- | --- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Aggregate belongs to eval-quality      | Run five qualified defect probes through the published engine path; inspect the engine-produced aggregate and its referenced per-probe evidence; assert TeA copied its bytes | Integration over real eval-quality | P0  | Local arithmetic or a missing engine artifact fails byte and lineage equality |
| Class floors govern held-out readiness | Four of five caught at floor `0.75` meets it; the same evidence at `0.9` does not; the guide reads only that engine decision                                                 | Integration, guidance              | P0  | Removing the floor comparison or changing either expected decision fails      |
| Null and comparability remain distinct | Exercise no eligible probe, an admitted but unexercised probe, and a trial set below `minimumTrialCount`; exclude controls and canaries from the denominator                 | Integration                        | P0  | Counting an excluded probe or converting a null or non-comparable state fails |
| Replay preserves the class result      | Replay the sealed inputs and compare the aggregate and lineage byte for byte; reject a missing or mismatched result                                                          | Replay                             | P0  | Replaying a changed artifact without a changed aggregate fails                |

Amended 2026-10-01 in the Story 1.45 build. The integration cases run in `test/test-evaluate-run.js` over a six-probe fixture (a clean control and five defect probes, each with its own mutation and witness, one left uncaught) and a two-probe fixture whose run directory is edited and re-anchored to reach the non-comparable and `rate: null` states. The replay row is a direct rerun of the recorded `aggregate-strength` argv with a fresh `--out`, whose bytes equal the copied aggregate. The mismatch rows run through `race-engine.js`, which tampers a persisted evidence file or forges the staged aggregate (digest, probe set, engine version, schema, bytes, a link). A canary cannot be driven through `tea-evaluate run`, so its denominator boundary is the engine's own and the TeA case covers the engine's refusal of a canary floor.

| Held-out score stays valid and closed     | Run development, then held-out; the probe is caught, its gap row is ID, class and outcome, rescoring after deleting the development run gives equal outcomes, `compare --accept` and `ci --tier pr` replay each baseline with no stale warning                                                                                                                                                                     | Live and replay     | P1  | Compiling `contract.json` for a held-out baseline reads as stale in `ci`                                   |
| Source bytes pass through                 | With no `partitionPlan`, the run's `contract.json` equals the folder's in every partition; the committed fixtures, baselines and replays stay byte-identical under `test:evaluate-authoring`, `test:evaluate-ci` and `test:evaluate-compare`                                                                                                                                                                       | Integration, replay | P0  | Re-serializing the contract changes a committed baseline byte                                              |
| `check` names paths and IDs only          | One case per rule (unknown step, ID collision or repeat, dangling `after` or pointer, view empties a behavior that had an oracle, non-deterministic evaluator, gameability probe, a waiver that reads a development-only step, empty `heldOutProbes`, invalid plan or schema); a canary-named binding key, `behaviorOracles` key, free-text step ID, oracle ID and `after` clause each leave no byte in the output | Integration         | P1  | A finding that quotes the plan or a rule left out fails its case                                           |

The both view gives a behavior its development and held-out oracles. Before Story 1.110 the probes of such a behavior were scored there with no designated oracle, so a probe read `caught: true` only through a finding that cites the contract's first-declared oracle; Story 1.110 hands each probe its own partition's oracle, and the Story 1.51 fixture asserted only that every probe was scored and that the probes of a one-oracle behavior read as in their own partition.

### Story 1.54: Reclaim auxiliary scratch after a killed preflight

Added in Story 1.28's second review. Levels: killed-process integration and reference assertion.

| AC                                               | Test                                                                                                                                                                                          | Level       | P   | Revert check                                      |
| ------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------- | --- | ------------------------------------------------- |
| Dead auxiliary scratch is reclaimed              | Kill real `preflight` during engine staging and real `run` during command evaluation; change `TMPDIR`, rerun preflight and assert each owned parent and scratch path was reported and removed | Integration | P1  | Disabling auxiliary recovery leaves each parent   |

| Auxiliary recovery is documented                 | Read the workspace reference section by heading and assert it names auxiliary scratch                                                                                                         | Static      | P2  | Deleting the passage fails the assertion          |

The edge-case check creates a recorded private root under an earlier temp base and drives the Windows recovery branch, while POSIX rejects that alternate root. Empty and prefix-only marker writes are reclaimed only when the parent contains no other entry; a linked recorded root and a partial marker beside another entry remain in place.

### Story 1.64: Hold the release across the witness legs and the trials

Added 2026-09-30 in Story 1.38. Levels: integration over real eval-quality, static. File: `test/test-evaluate-arms.js` (`test:evaluate-arms`).

| AC                                                    | Test                                                                                                                           | Level                              | P   | Revert check                                                             |
| ----------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------- | --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| A release changed after the witness legs refuses      | The pre-fix grader reports another release once the witness leg has run; assert the refusal naming both and no trial run       | Integration over real eval-quality | P0  | Dropping the request after the witness legs runs the trials              |
| A release changed during the trials refuses           | The pre-fix grader changes its release while the trials run; assert the refusal and that no trial set is sealed                | Integration over real eval-quality | P0  | Dropping the request after the trials seals trials under another release |
| An unchanged release is asked at each point           | Read the pre-fix server's request log: report, arms, witness leg, report, trials, report; assert the unchanged qualified probe | Integration                        | P1  | Asking only before the arms leaves two requests short                    |
| The later requests go through the port and the policy | A report operation the policy denies after the first request, and an answer with no string; assert each refusal and reason     | Integration                        | P1  | Sending them outside the port exits 0 with no refusal                    |
| The reference states the three points                 | Read `### Against deployments` under its heading                                                                               | Static                             | P2  | Removing the sentence fails the read                                     |

Amended 2026-10-01 in Story 1.65's build: each point asks every interface of the pre-fix deployment, so the request log of each of its origins shows three report requests, and the cases put the changed release on the second interface's origin as well as the first.

Amended 2026-10-02 in Story 1.64's build: the cases redeploy a pre-fix server on a signal the run sends (a test wrapper around the fixture's grader that changes the release it reports after a counted request) and read every server's request log.
The row "The later requests go through the port and the policy" reads: after the witness legs, the pre-fix grader redirects its report request to a host the registry does not authorize (eval-quality's `host-not-authorized` denies the hop) and the refusal names the point and that reason; an answer with no string is the second interface's origin reporting nothing (404); the unreachable row is a process that ends on the request (exit 12 naming the point); the secret row is a later answer that reports the auth value upper-cased.
A policy cannot deny the one report operation after allowing it once, so the redirect stands in for the denial; dropping the denial's refusal at a later point exits 0 with no refusal, which the case catches.
Each point is exercised at the first interface's origin, at the second interface's origin (the first asked and kept first) and over an arm that holds two probes, and a run whose only probe is refused after the legs or after the trials exits 12 with nothing sealed.

### Story 1.65: Ask every HTTP interface of a deployment which release it runs

Added 2026-09-30 in Story 1.38. Levels: integration over real eval-quality, contract, unit, static. Files: `test/test-evaluate-arms.js` (`test:evaluate-arms`), `test/test-evaluate-check.js` (`test:evaluate-check`).

| AC                                                       | Test                                                                                                                                          | Level                              | P   | Revert check                                                                       |
| -------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------- | --- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| A second origin on another release refuses               | Two HTTP interfaces, two fixture servers per deployment; the second origin reports another release; assert the refusal and each server's log  | Integration over real eval-quality | P0  | Asking the first interface alone lets the probe qualify                            |
| `check` and `deploymentPair` hold a report per interface | A report missing for an interface and one for an interface the registry does not serve, each exit 10 under `historical`; the unit's exit 12   | Contract, unit                     | P1  | Dropping the rule leaves an origin unasked                                         |
| The probe schema carries a report per interface          | A missing report and one with an extra or empty field under `schema`; a one-interface registry keeps its single report valid                  | Contract                           | P1  | Dropping the field lets a probe through with an origin unasked                     |
| The reference states that every origin is asked          | Read `### Against deployments` under its heading                                                                                              | Static                             | P2  | Removing the sentence fails the read                                               |
| `check` holds a report to the interface its key names    | A report keyed by the second interface that names the operation of the first; assert the finding names the interface the operation belongs to | Contract, unit                     | P1  | Dropping the rule lets the run ask an interface for an operation it does not serve |

Amended 2026-10-01 in Story 1.65's build: the two-interface registry, contract and policy live in the test's own project copy (`withLedger` over `makeDeploymentProject`), and `test/fixtures/evaluate-api` keeps its bytes.
The second interface is `ledger`, which sorts after `grader`, so each comparison falls on a later element.
Its servers are the fixture's grader behind a path prefix (`/ledger`), because eval-quality refuses two `api` operations that share a method and a path template and the grader answers `/release` alone.
There are four loopback servers, one per interface and deployment, each with its own request log.
The cases read the logs: every server receives one report request when each origin runs the declared release; the second pre-fix origin on another release refuses the probe naming `ledger` with the first origin asked once and the post-fix servers unasked; both pre-fix origins on another release refuse naming `grader` with the `reports` keys written `ledger` first, so the asking is in sorted order and the second origin stays unasked; the second post-fix origin on another release refuses naming the post-fix side after every earlier origin was asked once; a report operation of `ledger` the policy denies, an answer with no string at the pointer and the auth value echoed upper-cased each refuse naming `ledger`.
`check` cases name a report missing for the second interface (post-fix) and for the first (pre-fix), a report keyed by an interface the registry does not serve over HTTP, an operation of the first interface under the second key, and the schema cases for a missing, empty or old-shaped `reports` and an entry with a missing, extra or empty field; two clean cases keep a registry of one HTTP interface with one report and a registry of two with two valid.
`deploymentPair` and `reportsProblems` units cover the same rules.
`test-evaluate-arms.js --reported-interfaces-only` runs the interface cases alone for the revert checks.

Amended 2026-10-02 in Story 1.65's round 1 review: the `ledger` servers run the grader's `release: object` policy and `ledger` reports at `/release/name`, so a pointer read from the wrong interface's report finds an object or nothing (revert: reading the first interface's pointer for each fails 9 of 155 checks, 0 of 152 before).
A second-interface crash case asserts the exit 12 message names the `ledger` interface (revert: dropping the interface from the could-not-answer message fails 1), and the one-interface `deploymentPair` unit refuses a report for the second interface (revert: dropping the unserved-key rule fails 2).
The could-not-be-built message has no case, since `reportsProblems` refuses every operation the request cannot be built from.
Story 1.75 closes the collision of report paths across interfaces.

### Story 1.66: Scrub an observation in every letter case

Added 2026-09-30 in Story 1.38. Levels: integration over real eval-quality, unit. Files: `test/test-evaluate-api.js` (`test:evaluate-api`), `test/test-evaluate-arms.js` (`test:evaluate-arms`).

| AC                                                        | Test                                                                                                              | Level                              | P   | Revert check                                                          |
| --------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------- | --- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| An echoed secret is scrubbed in every letter case         | The grader echoes the injected auth value lowercased and uppercased in its body and a header; assert `[redacted]` | Integration over real eval-quality | P0  | Restoring the case-sensitive scrub leaves both echoes in              |
| A reported release echoing the secret reaches no artifact | A deployment reports a release holding the secret uppercased; assert `refused/<probeId>.json` and `run.json`      | Integration over real eval-quality | P1  | Restoring the case-sensitive scrub writes the secret into the refusal |
| The fault path and the observation scrub the same cases   | A fault quoting the secret uppercased; assert the message and cause                                               | Integration                        | P1  | Two different case sets let the uppercased echo through the fault     |
| A short value stays unscrubbed in every case              | A value under the minimum length echoed in each case; assert it stays                                             | Unit                               | P2  | Scrubbing it redacts ordinary text and fails the case                 |

Amended 2026-10-01 in Story 1.66's build: the first case echoes ten secrets (an ASCII value, a mixed-case one, one with pattern metacharacters, one with `ß` and letters with diacritics, one with a dotted capital I, one with a lone surrogate, one with letters outside the BMP, a base64 value, and one for the Turkish `i` and `I`, and Greek words ending in a capital sigma) in seven cases (as sent, lowercased, uppercased, capitalized per word, alternating, and the Turkish upper- and lower-casing), in a header, a body field inside other text, a nested array element and an object key, and in each byte format a serializer writes (plain, a JSON string body, `\uXXXX` in lower- and upper-case hex, Go's `<`, `>` and `&` escapes, `\/`, two levels of JSON); keys that scrub to one name are numbered. The registry-backed case beside the existing `leaky` case echoes the fixture's two values uppercased and capitalized. The third case runs each of those through a fault's message, its cause and the printed text it quotes, including a leading part the print cut off. The fourth case echoes three seven-character values (one with `/`, one with letters with diacritics) in every case and format beside an eight-character value that is scrubbed. More cases: ordinary text that merely resembles a secret stays; two secret sets on one port scrub apart; a cut text of four characters or more in any case is replaced and one of three stays, for a secret with `ß` and one with letters outside the BMP cut at every code unit; secrets that overlap in a text are replaced as one span, in either order; a credential file, a 2,520-character token and a 60,000-character secret scrub on the success path and on a fault whose own message survives; a number whose text holds `3456789E+25` is replaced, through the forms and through a hand-built array; the forms stay a longest-first array of strings and an empty form matches nothing. The revert check of the third case reads: the fault path scrubbing with the earlier case-sensitive code while the observation uses the new matching. In `test:evaluate-arms`, a deployment reports a release holding the secret uppercased (pre-fix side), capitalized inside a longer identifier (post-fix side) and lowercased beside an uppercased copy; each is refused with the identifier quoted as `[redacted]` and no file under the run directory and no output holds the value in any case. `test-evaluate-api.js --letter-cases-only` and `test-evaluate-arms.js --reported-releases-only` run these cases alone for the revert checks.

Amended 2026-10-01 in Story 1.66's round 1: the matrix adds a Turkish per-word capitalizer (`admin-index-token` echoed as `Admin-İndex-Token`), `münich-key-0123` and a secret ending in a letter outside the BMP, places each text echo at the middle, the end and the start of a text, and runs again on one port holding every secret behind an ASCII decoy. More cases: secret sets that share a value or differ only in how a comma splits their values, visited in both orders on one port; secrets that meet inside a character a fold expands (`ß`, a three-unit Greek fold) replaced as one run; cut texts after a length-changing fold; the longest-first order on a secret whose escaped forms are longer. Reverts: the dotted capital I folded by the generic path, the span inside an expanded character skipped, a forms memo keyed by part of the values, case variants of the first secret only, the upper-case variant dropped, a span end of one unit for a character, the cut position taken in folded units, and the forms left unsorted each fail a case.

A `regex` or `not-regex` pattern that `new RegExp` rejects or that is a `file://` reference (promptfoo compiles the file's content after the wrapper's check), and `weight: 0` (promptfoo then reports a failed assertion as a pass), are refused the same way, with `weight: 1` and `weight: 0.5` admitted.
The fourth row pins the ten specified types as a literal list that each file's export must equal, apart from the installed enumeration, and the two refusal blocks must be byte-identical.

Amended 2026-10-04 in Story 1.121's build: the three ci probes' witnesses read the request in the workflow the run wrote, so the ci direction is `plant-reported`, as for the other fifteen, and the `gap-read` label is gone from the kit.
P-001 and P-002 are a containment of `0 2 * * 0` and of `contents: read` with no `not`.
P-003 is an `all` of a containment of `npm test` and a `not` over a containment of `burn-in`: the positive operand keeps the witness silent on the alternate-platform leg, which writes no workflow and over which a `not` of a containment is true, so the flipped containment of `burn-in` alone still fails `seeded-faults-scoped`, and a bare containment of `burn-in` fires on the full project's run.
The ci pre-flight of all three passes from the leg cache with verdict null and exit 3 unchanged, and `expected-strength.json` moves in the three ci pre-flight records (their `basis` no longer names a failed pre-flight) and the ci corpus digest.
`test:ci-qualification` also resolves each ci witness over an absent workflow, and P-003's over the full project's correct pipeline, which pre-flight drops for P-001 and P-002 because their fault leg sends the `witness-github-actions` request; so those shapes are held without a leg cache.

### Story 1.122: Make the ci command oracles hold on the quoted stored capture

Added 2026-10-03 in Story 1.94's build. Levels: corpus, static. Files: `test/fixtures/ci-eval/ground-truth.json`, `tools/generate-contracts.js` (`ciOracleSpecs`, `ciContains`), `test/eval-ci.js` (`workflowMentions`, the element validation), `test/contracts/ci.contract.json`, `test/probes/ci.probes.json`, `test/probes/expected-strength.json`, `test/test-probe-corpus.js` (`KNOWN_UNHELD`).
The oracle machinery consumes `contractToken` as one literal (a `containment` operand and `String.includes`), so the quote-tolerant form is a `contractPattern` rendered with the vocabulary's `regex` operator and tested with `RegExp`; the operator rejects a nested quantifier and a pattern over its step budget.
The operator also accepts only a fully anchored pattern (`AnchoredPattern` in `eval-quality`'s `core/schemas/expression.js`: `^` first, `$` last; `regexRejection` in `core/compile/expression-legality.js` fails an unanchored one with `malformed-operator-expression`), so the patterns take the form `tokenGroupExpression` (`tools/generate-contracts.js`) already uses, `^[\s\S]*(?:<pattern>)[\s\S]*$`, and the paired scorer tests the same source with `new RegExp(source)` and no flags: `^[\s\S]*npm\s+install\s+--prefix\s+(?:'evals'|"evals"|evals)(?:\s|$)[\s\S]*$` and `^[\s\S]*tea-evaluate\s+ci\s+--evaluation\s+(?:'evals/grader'|"evals/grader"|evals/grader)\s+--tier\s+(?:'pr'|"pr"|pr)(?:\s|$)[\s\S]*$`.

| AC                                                           | Test                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Level  | P   | Revert check                                                              |
| ------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------ | --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Both command oracles hold on the stored capture              | `test:probe-corpus` scores the evaluation-plan project's stored workflow; O-031 and O-032 disposition `held`                                                                                                                                         | Corpus | P0  | Restoring the unquoted tokens fails `storedRunProblems` on both oracles   |
| The patterns tolerate quotes and pin the folder and tier     | The capture with double-quoted and unquoted folders passes; scratch copies with `--tier nightly`, `--tier prod`, `npm install --prefix other` and `--evaluation other/grader` each fail their oracle (`--tier nightly` and `--tier prod` fail O-032) | Corpus | P0  | A pattern without `--tier pr`, or with any prefix, leaves a copy passing  |
| Only the ci corpus digest moves in the baseline              | Regenerate, then compare `expected-strength.json` with `origin/main`: only the ci `corpusDigest` differs                                                                                                                                             | Corpus | P1  | A verdict, exit code or other record that moved fails the comparison      |
| `KNOWN_UNHELD` is empty                                      | `test:probe-corpus` asserts the list is empty and that an oracle which no longer holds fails with its id                                                                                                                                             | Static | P1  | A leftover entry fails once its oracle holds                              |
| A loosened pattern does not pass a workflow without it       | A scratch copy of a workflow that omits the install command fails O-031 through the paired scorer                                                                                                                                                    | Corpus | P1  | A pattern that matches any workflow leaves the omitted command passing    |
| P-004's record carries no `disposition-contradicts-evidence` | Score the clean control; O-031 and O-032 resolve `held` with corroboration `agrees` and the record names no contradiction                                                                                                                            | Corpus | P1  | Leaving the dispositions `violated` with no defect finding trips the rule |

Amended 2026-10-05 in Story 1.122's build, fix round 1: the patterns of the paragraph above replace the first ones, `['"]?evals['"]?` and `['"]?pr['"]?` between single spaces.
Each quote there was independent, so an unterminated quote, a closing quote only and a mismatched pair (`'evals"`) all held, and each is a shell syntax error.
Each argument is now a whole-word alternation, `(?:'x'|"x"|x)`, inside a group and with no nested quantifier, so only a balanced pair holds.
A folded YAML scalar (`run: >-`) can break the line between the words of a command and the harness accepts it, so the words are separated by `\s+`.
Amended 2026-10-05 in Story 1.122's build: the second row is the table of forty-one forms in `test:contract-oracles` (`checkCiCommandOraclesOnQuotedForms`), each resolved by the engine and by the paired scorer, and `validateCorpus` guards the `contractPattern` field (`checkCorpusGuards` in `test:evaluate-ci-render`).
The fourth row holds as `test:probe-corpus` failing a listed oracle that holds, which `knownUnheldProblems` exercises over an empty list, so a leftover entry fails and the check cannot be removed unseen.
The sixth row is `cleanControlProblems` in `test:probe-corpus`: every engine outcome of a clean control of the four stored-run suites is `held` with corroboration `agrees`, and `cleanControlSelfProblems` exercises it over a contradicted and a violated outcome.

an executable whose directory holds `=`; the vector's text before Bubblewrap (Node, the launcher, the arguments file, the environment file, with no `/bin/sh`, `-c`, `env`, `-u` or assignment) and the launcher's source holding no shell; Story 1.82's shell launcher, `env` restore included, as a control under dash and under bash as `sh`: it passes a row the shell leaves alone, dash drops `my.setting` and `BASH_FUNC_f%%` and bash as `sh` drops `PS1` and re-serializes `BASH_FUNC_f%%`; no file left in the status directory once a call that started twice has ended), `the socket launcher` (any host: descriptor 3 holds the arguments file; `/usr/bin/env` prints the call's environment byte for byte and in order, with a decimal integer name, and only the call's; the environment file's content and mode; the file removed once read and a bad one refusing the call with exit 126; the exit codes 7, 127 and 126 and the signal that ended the command (SIGTERM, and SIGPIPE and SIGXFSZ, which Node starts ignoring); each of SIGTERM, SIGINT, SIGHUP, SIGQUIT, SIGUSR1 and SIGUSR2 sent to the launcher reaching the command, with no debugger opened; the engine's group kill reaching the command; a 9 MB environment ending the launcher with exit 125 and its token, and a call that hides a socket and one that hides none each ending, through the engine's own command-line adapter, with the engine's `port-failure` fault whose reason is `launch-too-large`; a target that ran and ended with exit 125 and the token keeping its own exit, with no fault; the arguments file, the environment file and the status file removed when a write fails, the failed write leaving a created, partial file as a full disk does; a host's `NODE_V8_COVERAGE` kept out of the command; a `NODE_OPTIONS` of the call running in the command alone; the audited vector in the order launcher, `strace`, Bubblewrap, run through stand-ins that record what each was given; the shim's `startEnvironment`), `the path socket route` (Linux: `/usr/bin/env` through the real launcher, Bubblewrap and shim prints the same bytes with hidden sockets and with none, `PWD` being the directory Bubblewrap sets for every call) and the claims of `the network reference`.

### Story 1.130: Retry and verify the actionlint download so a GitHub outage cannot fail a shard

Added 2026-10-03 in Story 1.82's merge. Levels: static, integration. Files: `tools/install-actionlint.sh`, `test/test-install-actionlint.js` (`test:install-actionlint`), `tools/validate-ci-coverage.js` (`test:ci-coverage`) and `test/test-ci-coverage.js` (`test:ci-coverage-filters`). The integration case runs the installer, which both workflows call, against a stub server on any host with bash, curl, gzip and tar, and is skipped with its reason named where one is missing.

| AC                                          | Test                                                                                                                                                                   | Level       | P   | Revert check                                                  |
| ------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------- | --- | ------------------------------------------------------------- |
| The download retries a transient failure    | A stub answers 503 twice and then a real gzip tarball; the step ends with actionlint installed after three attempts                                                    | Integration | P1  | A step with no retry fails on the first 503                   |
| A body that is no gzip file is not unpacked | A stub answers an HTML error page; the step retries, and when every attempt fails it exits non-zero without calling `tar` on the body                                  | Integration | P1  | A step that extracts without `gzip -t` runs `tar` on the page |
| The final failure names its cause           | The last error names the attempts made, the last HTTP status and that the body was no gzip file                                                                        | Integration | P2  | The bare `gzip: stdin: not in gzip format` fails the case     |
| Both workflows keep the retry and the check | Read `quality.yaml` and `publish.yaml` for the retry, the tarball check and the floating version; the version is not pinned and the download script stays hash-checked | Static      | P1  | Dropping any of the three from either file fails the check    |

Amended 2026-10-04 in Story 1.130's build: the retry and the checks live in `tools/install-actionlint.sh`, which both workflows call, so the integration rows run the installer against the stub (the stub serves a stand-in for the pinned script with its `curl -L "$url" | tar xvz` contract, and its `tar` is the real one) and the static row reads both workflows for the call and the installer for the rest.
The integration file also holds an outage of about two minutes (the stub answers 503 for the first six tarball requests, and the installer's waits of 2, 4, 8, 16, 32 and 64 seconds, recorded through the sleep seam, carry it to the seventh), the latest-version resolution (the stub's `/releases/latest` redirects to `v9.9.9` and the tarball path asks for 9.9.9; the resolution retries), a truncated tarball, a cut connection, a checksum mismatch, a missing checksum file that skips the checksum check alone, a checksum file with no line for the tarball (one tarball request), the pinned script's digest, and the wait of 2, 4, 8, 16, 32 and 64 seconds within a 126 second budget at the production default base and the 170 second deadline.
The static row's version check holds because the installer resolves the latest tag itself: the pinned script's `latest` keyword downloads the version built into it.
The static row also holds the production defaults (attempts, wait budget, wait base, sleep command, deadline) and `timeout-minutes: 5` on both workflow steps.
Revert checks as observed: a single attempt fails 9 of 10 cases of `test:install-actionlint`, the two-minute outage among them (the schedule of the first build, five attempts and a 60 second budget, fails 8 of 10, the same case among them); `gzip -t` removed fails 3 of 10 (the HTML body reaches the checksum check, which refuses it with the wrong cause, and the page reaches the stand-in's `tar` when the release has no checksum file); the checksum comparison removed fails 1 of 10; the bare `gzip: stdin: not in gzip format` as the final message fails 5 of 10; a hard-coded version fails 3 of 10 and the static check; each workflow reverted to the inline `curl ... | bash` step fails the static check, one run per file; a retry base default of 0 fails 2 of 10, and a sleep default of `true`, a deadline default of 2000 and a step timeout of 30 each fail the static check.

| ----------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------- | --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| A killed run leaves no call directory in the temp directory | Kill a run with `SIGKILL` while a call is open, run again over the evaluation: no `tea-evaluate-target-tmp-*`, `tea-evaluate-port-*` or `tea-nb-*` remains and the output names the reclaimed parent | Integration | P1  | A call directory made in the system's temp directory again fails the case |

| The golden and the reference state the new places           | Read the golden's diff and `### File-system confinement`                                                                                                                                             | Static      | P2  | Leaving the old sentence fails the case                                   |

Amended 2026-10-06 in Story 1.131's build: the rows are run by these cases, and the Linux cases run in the ubuntu CI job and are skipped elsewhere with their reason named.
The first row is `the call directories of a killed run` (`test/test-evaluate-api.js`, every host: `test/fixtures/evaluate/killed-call.cjs` makes the three directories through the layer's own code and is killed with `SIGKILL`; the temp directory holds none of them before and after the second run's preflight, and the output names the reclaimed parent and each directory; the same run plants links, a hard link, a closed directory and record-shaped files in them and forges the marker and the journal record, and the canaries outside stay whole) and the port directory of a real preflight killed mid-call (`a started service's port`); `the call directory units` holds each mechanism's choice of directory.
The temp directory made in the system's temp directory again fails `the call directory units` (`callTemporary`), `a confined target's temp directory`, `the call directories of a killed run` and `the call directories, stood in`.
The port directory made there again fails `the call directory units` (`makePortDirectory`), `the call directories of a killed run`, `the call directories, stood in` and `a started service's port`.
The bridge directory made there again fails `the call directory units` (`makeBridgeDirectory`), `the call directories of a killed run` and `the call directories, stood in`.
The second row is `the call directory units` (the vector on every host: each directory bound writable at `/dev/<name>` after the private root is emptied and at no other path, the environment, the port file and `--bridge` naming the mount, the audit's read and write grants holding the mounts, the Seatbelt profile allowing each directory again after the denial), `the call directories, stood in` (a stand-in for Bubblewrap that applies each bind under `/dev` over the real sandbox, mechanisms, shim and HTTP port: the port file is read and the bridge answers at the directory's own path, `TMPDIR` is writable) and, in the ubuntu job, `the call directory route` (real Bubblewrap, with a control that binds the directories at their own paths and finds them hidden) and the confined pipeline, whose audit lists none of them; binding a directory at a path the sandbox hides fails the vector case, the stand-in case and the route case.
The third row is `the isolation golden` (`test:isolation-primitives`, four added entries and, from review round 1, the `env` prefix on the eight Seatbelt target entries) and `the network reference`, whose stale-sentence check fails while the old sentence that placed the granted directories outside the private root remains and whose six claims name the cases above.
Amended 2026-10-07 in Story 1.131's review round 1: two rows more.
A killed run leaves no observer probe in the system's temp directory: `the observer probe of a killed run` (`test/test-evaluate-api.js`, every host that confines) kills a real confined preflight while `observer-probe-<pid>-<random>` exists beneath the private root, finds the temp directory empty and the next preflight removing and naming the entry, and leaves a forged entry (a link, a file, a directory of another mode, a directory of another owner, a directory of a live pid, a name of another shape, a name with a prefix) and the canary a link names whole; making the probe in the system's temp directory again fails it, and so does leaving the recovery out of `preflight`.
A confined Seatbelt target loses the host's `NODE_V8_COVERAGE`: `a confined target's temp directory` runs its `evaluate` with the variable set to a scratch directory, and taking the `env -u NODE_V8_COVERAGE` prefix out of the Seatbelt vector fails it whatever the harness exports; `the call directory units` and the golden hold the vector.

### Story 1.92: Stop `tea-evaluate ci` at once on a signal while an engine stage runs

Added 2026-10-01 in Story 2.2's review. Levels: integration, static. File: `test/test-evaluate-ci.js` (`test:evaluate-ci`).

Amended 2026-10-06 in Story 1.92's build: four cases carry the story. `a signal while an engine stage runs` repeats the case for the replay's `score`, `aggregate-strength` and `preflight`, the stale-baseline compile (inside `replay` and after a plan that does not read the baseline), a plan's `compile` and `seal` and the `check` check's compile, over a stage that hangs (the kill shim's `KILL_HOW=hang`). `a signal while an engine stage writes` covers four stages (the replay's `score` and `preflight`, a plan's `compile` and the `check` check's compile) over a stage that keeps creating and filling its `--out` directory and ignores SIGINT and SIGTERM (`KILL_HOW=write`). Each signals `ci` with SIGINT and SIGTERM, stops no stage itself, and asserts the end by the signal within a bound, the stage's pid gone, no `run-<ci pid>-*` entry and no `tea-evaluate-*` entry in the temporary directory. `a signal to a command that runs a stage` holds `check`, `preflight`, `run` and `score` to the same end, and `a killed check check` holds the SIGKILL of a `ci` whose `check` check compiles.

| AC                                                        | Test                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Level       | P   | Revert check                                                                                                           |
| --------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------- | --- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| A signal ends `ci` while the replay's stage hangs         | Run `ci --tier pr` over the verdict fixture with the kill shim hanging at `score`; send SIGINT, then SIGTERM, to `ci` and do not kill the stage; assert `ci` ends by the signal                                                                                                                                                                           | Integration | P0  | Reverting `runEngineStage` to `spawnSync` fails on the timeout                                                         |
| The stage and the scratch directory are gone              | After the signal, assert the stage's pid is gone and the temporary directory holds no `tea-evaluate-*` entry                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Integration | P0  | A handler that leaves the scratch directory fails the empty listing                                                    |
| The same for `compile`, `seal` and the stale-baseline run | Hang each stage in turn and repeat the two assertions                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Integration | P1  | A stage still called synchronously fails its case                                                                      |
| Stage exits still pass through verbatim                   | The existing `checkEngineStageExits` and shim-log cases run unchanged                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Integration | P0  | An exit rewritten on the asynchronous path fails the direct-CLI check                                                  |
| A writing stage does not outlive the removal              | Run `a signal while an engine stage writes` over the kill shim in `KILL_HOW=write` mode (four stages: the replay's `score` and `preflight`, a plan's `compile`, the `check` check's compile), which keeps creating and filling the directory of its `--out` and ignores SIGINT and SIGTERM; assert no `run-<pid>-*` and no `tea-evaluate-*` entry is left | Integration | P0  | A handler that removes the scratch before it stops the stage finds the stage's new entries                             |
| Every command that runs a stage ends it on a signal       | Run `check` (two interfaces), `preflight`, `run` and `score` with the stage hanging or writing; send SIGINT, then SIGTERM, to the command and do not kill the stage; assert the end by the signal, the stage's pid gone and no private or temporary entry                                                                                                 | Integration | P1  | A command without `cleanUpOnSignal` (or a handler that does not stop the stage) leaves the stage or its directory      |
| A killed `check` check is reclaimed                       | Hang the `check` check's compile over a two-interface contract, SIGKILL `ci`, then the stage, and run `ci` again                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Integration | P1  | A check that takes no invocation scratch directory leaves its parent without an owner file, and the next run leaves it |

### Story 1.94: Score each stored workflow in the CI probe leg

Added 2026-10-01 in Story 2.3's review. Levels: static, replay. Files: `test/lib/probe-scoring.js`, `test/test-probe-corpus.js` (`test:probe-corpus`), `tools/generate-contracts.js` (`ciOracleSpecs`).

Amended 2026-10-03 in Story 1.94: the work covers the trace, nfr, test-design and ci builders and the two test-review oracles that read the stored verdict, and `test:probe-corpus` carries the checks that fail on a revert (`storedRunProblems`, `wrongRunProblems`, the unknown-oracle check and the test-review verdict check), so the wrong-row observation is a permanent case as well as a scratch-copy one. `wrongRunProblems` reads each set through the next set's run, each leg through every other stored case, and a refused run on every leg, and requires every oracle that holds on its own run to fail under one of them (a violation a refusal produced counts only for the `run-measured` oracles, since the refusal answers every other oracle without calling its scorer); the three ci `run-measured` oracles, whose scorer reads no workflow, are listed as unfailable, and the two test-design projection-coherence oracles fail only through the `projectionOf` read that drops the `design` key. `storedRunExposureProblems` fails a suite of `STORED_RUN_SUITES` whose builder exposes no `storedRunSpecs` or `storedRunLegs`, so a builder reverted to a constant `held` cannot opt out. A row pointed at a stored deviation fails for 11 of the 34 constructed ci deviations and passes through for 23, filed as Story 1.123.

| `expected-strength.json` is unchanged                     | `test:probe-corpus` over the committed file; two oracles the real capture fails are listed in `KNOWN_UNHELD` and fail the check once they hold                                                                                                   | Static | P1  | A scorer that disagrees with a correct workflow fails `storedRunProblems`                                                    |
| Other suites' builders read a stored run through a scorer | Search `test/lib/probe-scoring.js` for fixed dispositions over a stored run; trace, nfr and test-design are scored, test-review measures its stored verdicts (payload and exit code included), fragment selection and routing read no stored run | Static | P2  | A builder left on a constant fails its `wrongRunProblems` case                                                               |

Two constructed deviations are stored beside the live capture, each one edit of it, one that misses the wait alone (`evaluation-gate-needs-cut`) and one that misses the release job and the wait (`evaluation-gate-release-job-on-pull-requests`): two cases that miss the same elements must carry the same detail, so the other single edits are scored in `test:evaluate-ci-render`.

The gate block of the template and its parse test carry the workflow path beside the conclusion and the event.
The stored live capture is held to rule C by `test:evaluate-ci-render` (every event the publish job's `if:` lets through is one the release evaluation job's `if:` lets through), and the test's own helper applies section 2 and items 7 and 10 to the two committed live repositories (the `needs` wait, the guards, the widened triggers, and the conflicts and refusals from edits of them), and the `wait` element holds the events the gated job still runs on.

### Story 1.98: The AI-feature evaluation passes its own CI tiers

Added 2026-10-01 in Story 2.4's first review. Levels: integration. Files: `test/fixtures/evaluate-authoring/ai-feature/evaluation/`, `test/fixtures/evaluate-ci-repos/*/evals/answer-grade/`, `test/test-evaluate-ci.js`, `test/test-evaluate-authoring.js`.

Amended 2026-10-04 in Story 1.98's build: the repository tiers run in `test/test-evaluate-ci-repositories.js`, split into the seven chained scripts `test:evaluate-ci-repositories:<adopter>-<tier>` (see the CI round 1 amendment below), chained after `test:evaluate-ci`, since their live tiers take several minutes per repository and `test:evaluate-ci` alone weighs 434 seconds in the shard weights. `test:evaluate-ci` keeps the capture-record guard, which reverses a declared Story 1.98 migration of each repository's `evaluation.json`. `test:evaluate-authoring` holds, for the AI-feature and the test-review replay, that every oracle outcome of the scored trials reads `agrees`, that in every scored trial the oracles a mutation violates belong to exactly the behaviors its defects declare, that a degenerate answer violates only its own behavior's oracle, and that every declared floor has an eligible probe in the development and the held-out partition. The violated oracles come from the oracle outcomes of the scored evidence, since the qualification evidence lists only the declared behaviors' oracles. Two assertions per trial name the difference from the declaration, a violated behavior the probe does not declare and a declared behavior whose oracles the trial leaves held, each printing `trial n violates the oracles of [..] where the probe declares [..]`, and the corroboration assertion runs after them. `test:evaluate-guidance` holds the starter's `defect` floor, the corpus guide's floor rules and, for each of the six kinds, an eligible non-clean probe of every starter floor class in the worked development partition and in the worked held-out list, and `test:evaluate-gap-loop` accounts for the Story 1.98 files of the test-review evaluation it copies. Each check fails over the pre-repair bytes of `origin/main`, by name.

Amended 2026-10-04 in Story 1.79's CI round 1: the suite first ran its seven tier runs (tagged-release `pr`, `merge`, `release`; nightly-deploy `pr`, `merge`, `scheduled`, `release`) in one script, about 1137 CI seconds against a weight of 530, and chain shard 3 of 12 timed out at 20 minutes. `--only=<adopter>:<tier>` now selects exactly one tier of the adopter named, each run uses a copy of the repository of its own, every run holds the adopter-level assertions, and a selector that selects nothing fails. Each tier is its own script with a weight from its measured CI seconds, so the shard planner spreads them. Every invocation first holds `select` to its contract and holds the seven scripts to the suite's adopter x tier pairs (each runs its own pair, sits in the `npm test` chain once and carries a weight). `test:evaluate-ci-repositories` stays as the all-tiers command and is not chained.

| Every class without an eligible probe has its floor removed or a probe added | the `merge`, `scheduled` and `release` scripts of `test:evaluate-ci-repositories` run `ci --tier merge`, `scheduled` and `release` over each repository (release exited 2 in both before) | Integration | P0  | Restoring the floor with no eligible probe exits 2                                       |
